@@ -0,0 +1,462 @@
+"""SPDX-License: MIT; Project VUT; (C) Frank-Rene Schaefer
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+PURPOSE
+       THE BOOKKEEPER -- one test directory: what a test IS, what it
+       HAS DONE, and THE NAMING of every file that carries it.
+
+DESCRIPTION
+       ONE BASE: 'GOOD/result_db.json'. It records properly; there is
+       nothing to bless about it. The file is write-protected as every
+       other file in GOOD -- a guard against careless hands. This
+       adapter is the ONLY way in or out: it unprotects, writes
+       atomically, re-protects.
+
+       THE NAMING. A record's key is (test, choice, subject); the
+       Bookkeeper turns it into the file that carries it -- nominal
+       under 'GOOD/', candidate under '.hwut-store/', the raw and cadence
+       sidecars beside the candidate. Whoever reads or writes those
+       files asks here for the path; the reading and writing themselves
+       are the caller's.
+
+       THE ENTRIES. One entry per (test, choice, operation),
+       OVERWRITTEN -- state now, never a log; what was done before is
+       the concern of the software configuration management system. The
+       Bookkeeper DERIVES an entry from the result, the configuration
+       and the goal: verdict and report from the result, both halves of
+       freeing (canonicaliser, compare setup) from the choice's
+       configuration, 'when' and 'host' added here, and THE ATTRIBUTION
+       -- the record of every process that produced the result -- made
+       durable beside them.
+
+       THE REPRODUCTION. Each write refreshes the test's and the
+       choice's reproducible configuration in the base, so a query can
+       answer what a test is configured to do from the base alone.
+
+       THE DIVERGENCE is detected ON CALL. The caller hands in what is
+       DECLARED; the Bookkeeper answers what is recorded but declared
+       no longer: a test reads DELETED, a choice NON-RESPONSIVE. What
+       either verdict means is the caller's -- under 'run all tests' it
+       is an error, under 'run this test with that choice' it is not.
+       Healing is a service (rename, rename-choice, remove,
+       remove-choice), never a guess.
+
+       A missing or damaged base reads as EMPTY: the base is a record,
+       and its loss must never fail a run.
+______________________________________________________________________________
+"""
+import json
+import os
+import platform
+import stat
+from   dataclasses import fields, is_dataclass
+from   datetime    import datetime, timezone
+from   enum        import Enum
+from   pathlib     import Path
+from .configuration import E_StderrNote, NamingConfig
+
+
+RESULT_DB_FILE_NAME = "result_db.json"
+
+NO_CHOICE_KEY       = "<none>"
+
+_OPERATION_BY_GOAL  = {"VERDICT": "Run",
+                       "DISPLAY": "Display",
+                       "NOMINAL": "Accept"}
+
+E_DIVERGENCE_DELETED        = "deleted"
+E_DIVERGENCE_NON_RESPONSIVE = "non-responsive"
+
+
+def this_host():
+    """
+    RETURN: str, a host tag for an entry, 'linux-x86_64/<node>'.
+
+    Coarse on purpose: it exists to compare compute speed between hosts,
+    not to identify a machine.
+    """
+    return "%s-%s/%s" % (platform.system().lower(), platform.machine(),
+                         platform.node())
+
+
+def _now():
+    """RETURN: str, the current UTC instant, seconds resolution, ISO."""
+    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
+
+
+def _plain(value):
+    """
+    RETURN: the value if JSON can carry it; an Enum's name; a
+            dataclass's fields, each made plain; the repr otherwise.
+
+    The base is read by anything, so nothing Python-shaped may reach it.
+    """
+    if isinstance(value, (str, int, float, bool, type(None))): return value
+    if isinstance(value, Enum):           return value.name
+    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):  return [_plain(v) for v in value]
+    if isinstance(value, dict):
+        return {str(k): _plain(v) for k, v in value.items()}
+    if is_dataclass(value):
+        return {f.name: _plain(getattr(value, f.name))
+                for f in fields(value)}
+    return repr(value)
+
+
+def compare_setup_delta(options):
+    """
+    RETURN: dict, every compare setting that DIFFERS from the default.
+            {},   the setup is compare's default throughout.
+
+    ONLY THE DIFFERENCES. A default setup adds nothing to an entry,
+    and recording the whole of compare's Configuration would make every
+    entry grow whenever compare gained an option. What is worth
+    keeping is what somebody CHOSE.
+
+    Nothing here names a tolerance: the walk is over whatever compare
+    DECLARES, so a tolerance compare has not invented yet is recorded the
+    day it is used. The declaration is read through 'dataclasses.fields'
+    -- the same source compare's own defaults stand in, so the delta and
+    the defaults cannot disagree about what a member is.
+    """
+    if options is None: return {}
+    from ...compare.configuration import Configuration
+    default    = Configuration()
+    difference = {}
+
+    for field in fields(Configuration):
+        name   = field.name
+        chosen = getattr(options, name, None)
+        plain  = getattr(default, name, None)
+        if name == "pattern_finder":
+            for inner in fields(plain):
+                a = getattr(chosen, inner.name, None)
+                b = getattr(plain,  inner.name, None)
+                if a != b: difference[inner.name] = _plain(a)
+            continue
+        if chosen != plain:
+            difference[name] = _plain(chosen)
+    return difference
+
+
+class Bookkeeper:
+    """ONE test directory's book: the naming, the entries, the
+    reproducible configurations, and the divergence verdicts.
+
+    THE NAMING LAW lives in 'configuration.py' (NamingConfig):
+    'same_nominal_f' drops the choice part from a NOMINAL's key, so
+    every choice is held against one blessed file; candidates keep
+    their own names, so a diff still names which choice diverged.
+
+    Made ABOVE -- the orchestrator makes one from the test's directory
+    and hands it down; a service that needs one makes it inside the
+    service. Nothing below makes its own.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, directory, naming=None):
+        """
+        RETURN: Bookkeeper over 'directory'.
+
+        'naming' is the NamingConfig in force; None takes the default
+        (a nominal per choice). It reaches the NOMINAL's name only --
+        candidates are always named per choice.
+        """
+        self.directory = Path(directory)
+        self.naming    = naming if naming is not None else NamingConfig()
+
+    # -- the naming ---------------------------------------------------
+    def key(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """
+        RETURN: str, the file name of one record: 'test--choice.subject'
+                for a test with choices, 'test.subject' without.
+        """
+        stem = test if choice is None else "%s--%s" % (test, choice)
+        return "%s.%s" % (stem, subject)
+
+    def nominal_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """
+        RETURN: Path, where the ACCEPTED record of that key lives.
+
+        Under 'same_nominal_f' the CHOICE PART IS DROPPED: every choice
+        of the test is held against one blessed file. Candidates keep
+        their own names regardless, or the choices would overwrite one
+        another (configuration.py, NamingConfig).
+        """
+        nominal_choice = None if self.naming.same_nominal_f else choice
+        return self.directory / "GOOD" / self.key(test, nominal_choice,
+                                                  subject)
+
+    def candidate_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the CANDIDATE record of that key lives.
+
+        THE STORE'S OWN GROUND, apart from 'OUT/': 'OUT/' is the
+        TEST'S product space -- execution reads every file there as a
+        subject -- and the store's records must never become the next
+        run's subjects. The dot-name also keeps the tree walk out."""
+        return self.directory / ".hwut-store" \
+                              / self.key(test, choice, subject)
+
+    def raw_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the PRE-canonicalisation stream lives."""
+        path = self.candidate_path(test, choice, subject)
+        return path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".raw")
+
+    def timing_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the cadence sidecar of that key lives."""
+        path = self.candidate_path(test, choice, subject)
+        return path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".times")
+
+    # -- the base -----------------------------------------------------
+    @property
+    def result_db_path(self):
+        """RETURN: Path, the ONE base of this directory."""
+        return self.directory / "GOOD" / RESULT_DB_FILE_NAME
+
+    def book(self):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the whole base -- test -> {'configuration',
+                'choices'}. Empty when nothing was recorded.
+
+        A missing or unreadable base reads as empty: the base is a
+        record, and its loss must never fail a run.
+        """
+        try:
+            with open(self.result_db_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+                content = json.load(fh)
+        except Exception:
+            return {}
+        return content if isinstance(content, dict) else {}
+
+    def _write_book(self, content):
+        """
+        RETURN: None. The whole base, replaced atomically and left
+                write-protected.
+
+        Unprotect, write beside, replace, re-protect: a reader never
+        meets a half-written base, and a careless hand never meets a
+        writable one.
+        """
+        path = self.result_db_path
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        if path.exists():
+            os.chmod(path, stat.S_IRUSR | stat.S_IWUSR
+                           | stat.S_IRGRP | stat.S_IROTH)
+        temporary = path.with_suffix(".json.tmp")
+        with open(temporary, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+            json.dump(content, fh, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
+            fh.write("\n")
+        os.replace(temporary, path)
+        os.chmod(path, stat.S_IRUSR | stat.S_IRGRP | stat.S_IROTH)
+
+    # -- recording ----------------------------------------------------
+    def record(self, result, configuration, goal, choice_name=None):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the entry as written -- 'when' and 'host' are
+                added here, so no caller has to remember them.
+
+        DERIVED, not handed in: the verdict and the report token from
+        the result; both halves of freeing from the choice's
+        configuration (the canonicaliser changed the RECORD, the
+        compare setup changed the VERDICT -- without them a later
+        reader cannot tell what this entry meant); THE ATTRIBUTION from
+        the result's provision -- the record of the process that
+        produced a result is PART of that result, in every mode, and
+        this is its durable home.
+
+        OVERWRITES exactly one (test, choice, operation) entry and
+        leaves every other untouched; refreshes the test's and the
+        choice's reproducible configuration beside it.
+
+        THE LEDGER READING of an ACCEPT (n-3): the book is a book of
+        record, and an accept is an EVENT in it. The entry keeps
+        'first_accept' -- the instant of the FIRST acceptance ever,
+        carried forward untouched -- while 'when' is the LAST: a
+        re-accept moves only the second.
+        """
+        operation = _OPERATION_BY_GOAL[goal.name]
+        entry     = {"verdict": bool(result.verdict),
+                     "report":  str(result.report),
+                     "when":    _now(),
+                     "host":    this_host()}
+        choice_entry = configuration.choice_configuration(choice_name)
+        if choice_entry.canonicalisers:
+            entry["canonicaliser"] = {name: list(argv) for name, argv
+                                      in choice_entry.canonicalisers.items()}
+        setup = compare_setup_delta(choice_entry.compare)
+        if setup:
+            entry["compare"] = setup
+        records = tuple(getattr(result.provision, "records", ()) or ())
+        if records:
+            entry["records"] = [_plain(r) for r in records]
+
+        content    = self.book()
+        test_book  = content.setdefault(result.name, {})
+        test_book["configuration"] = self._test_facts(configuration)
+        choice_key = NO_CHOICE_KEY if choice_name is None else choice_name
+        choice_db  = test_book.setdefault("choices", {})
+        choice_book = choice_db.setdefault(choice_key, {})
+        choice_book["configuration"] = self._choice_facts(choice_entry)
+        operation_db = choice_book.setdefault("operations", {})
+        if operation == "Accept":
+            prior = operation_db.get("Accept")
+            entry["first_accept"] = prior.get("first_accept",
+                                              prior.get("when")) \
+                                    if prior else entry["when"]
+        operation_db[operation] = entry
+        self._write_book(content)
+        return entry
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _test_facts(configuration):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the test application's reproducible facts:
+                source file and kind, interpreter, interactive, caps.
+        """
+        facts = {"source_file": configuration.source_file,
+                 "source_kind": str(configuration.source_kind),
+                 "interactive": bool(configuration.interactive)}
+        if configuration.interpreter is not None:
+            facts["interpreter"] = list(configuration.interpreter)
+        if configuration.caps is not None:
+            facts["caps"] = _plain(configuration.caps)
+        return facts
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _choice_facts(choice_entry):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the choice's reproducible facts: the
+                canonicalisers and the compare setup's differences.
+                Empty facts are absent, not empty.
+        """
+        facts = {}
+        if choice_entry.canonicalisers:
+            facts["canonicaliser"] = {name: list(argv) for name, argv
+                                      in choice_entry.canonicalisers.items()}
+        setup = compare_setup_delta(choice_entry.compare)
+        if setup:
+            facts["compare"] = setup
+        return facts
+
+    # -- queries ------------------------------------------------------
+    def tests(self):
+        """RETURN: list, every recorded test's name, sorted."""
+        return sorted(self.book())
+
+    def choices(self, test):
+        """
+        RETURN: list, every recorded choice of that test, sorted; None
+                stands where the test ran without a choice.
+        """
+        recorded = self.book().get(test, {}).get("choices", {})
+        return sorted((None if key == NO_CHOICE_KEY else key
+                       for key in recorded),
+                      key=lambda name: (name is not None, name))
+
+    def stderr_note(self, test, choice):
+        """
+        RETURN: E_StderrNote, what the book says about that choice's
+                stderr:
+
+                    IGNORED    whatever happens there, do not worry
+                    FORBIDDEN  a word there is an ERROR
+
+                An unnoted choice reads FORBIDDEN: a test that was
+                never asked about stderr is one that has never spoken
+                there, and the first word it says is news. A book
+                written before E-5 may still hold 'nominal' -- unknown
+                to this enum, so it reads FORBIDDEN too: the migration
+                is silent, and a speaking choice is caught at its next
+                run rather than trusted on old say-so.
+        """
+        key   = NO_CHOICE_KEY if choice is None else choice
+        noted = self.book().get(test, {}).get("choices", {}) \
+                           .get(key, {}).get("stderr")
+        try:
+            return E_StderrNote(noted)
+        except ValueError:
+            return E_StderrNote.FORBIDDEN
+
+    def note_stderr(self, test, choice, note):
+        """
+        RETURN: E_StderrNote, what now stands in the book for that
+                choice -- written verbatim, replacing any earlier note.
+
+        THE NOTE IS THE DECISION, and acceptance is where it is taken.
+        STDERR IS NEVER SUBJECT TO TESTING (E-5): noting IGNORED or
+        FORBIDDEN removes any nominal stderr that lingers from before
+        this ruling, since a stream cannot be both compared and
+        disregarded.
+        """
+        note       = E_StderrNote(note)
+        content    = self.book()
+        test_book  = content.setdefault(test, {})
+        choice_db  = test_book.setdefault("choices", {})
+        key        = NO_CHOICE_KEY if choice is None else choice
+        choice_db.setdefault(key, {})["stderr"] = note.value
+        self._write_book(content)
+        #  STDERR IS NEVER SUBJECT TO TESTING (E-5): every remaining
+        #  note -- IGNORED or FORBIDDEN -- means no nominal stderr can
+        #  stand, so any that lingers is removed, unconditionally.
+        path = self.nominal_path(test, choice, "stderr")
+        if path.exists(): path.unlink()
+        return note
+
+    def result(self, test, choice, operation):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the most recent entry of that operation.
+                None, no such entry.
+        """
+        key = NO_CHOICE_KEY if choice is None else choice
+        return self.book().get(test, {}).get("choices", {}) \
+                          .get(key, {}).get("operations", {}).get(operation)
+
+    def test_configuration(self, test):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the test's reproducible facts as last recorded.
+                None, the test is not in the book.
+        """
+        return self.book().get(test, {}).get("configuration")
+
+    def choice_configuration(self, test, choice):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the choice's reproducible facts as last recorded.
+                None, the choice is not in the book.
+        """
+        key = NO_CHOICE_KEY if choice is None else choice
+        return self.book().get(test, {}).get("choices", {}) \
+                          .get(key, {}).get("configuration")
+
+    # -- divergence, ON CALL -------------------------------------------
+    def divergence(self, declared_db):
+        """
+        RETURN: dict, the verdicts on what is recorded but declared no
+                longer:
+                    'deleted'        -> [test, ...]
+                    'non-responsive' -> {test: [choice, ...]}
+                Both empty when book and declaration agree.
+
+        'declared_db' maps each declared test's name to its declared
+        choice names (None where the test runs without a choice). A
+        recorded test absent from it reads DELETED; a recorded choice
+        of a declared test absent from its declaration reads
+        NON-RESPONSIVE. A declared name never yet recorded raises no
+        complaint -- it is simply new.
+
+        Healing is a service (rename, rename-choice, remove,
+        remove-choice); this answer never edits the book.
+        """
+        deleted        = []
+        non_responsive = {}
+        for test, test_book in sorted(self.book().items()):
+            if test not in declared_db:
+                deleted.append(test)
+                continue
+            declared = {NO_CHOICE_KEY if c is None else c
+                        for c in declared_db[test]}
+            missing  = sorted(key for key in test_book.get("choices", {})
+                              if key not in declared)
+            if missing:
+                non_responsive[test] = \
+                    [None if key == NO_CHOICE_KEY else key
+                     for key in missing]
+        return {E_DIVERGENCE_DELETED:        deleted,
+                E_DIVERGENCE_NON_RESPONSIVE: non_responsive}
diff --git a/orchestrator/bookkeeper/configuration.py b/orchestrator/bookkeeper/configuration.py
new file mode 100644
--- /dev/null
+++ b/orchestrator/bookkeeper/configuration.py
@@ -0,0 +1,103 @@
+"""SPDX-License: MIT; Project VUT; (C) Frank-Rene Schaefer
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+PURPOSE: THE BOOKKEEPER'S CONFIGURATION -- what is kept, where, and
+         under what name.
+
+Two configurations live here: 'StoreConfig', the storing knobs a test
+carries verbatim, and 'NamingConfig', the law by which a record's file
+name is formed. They are separate because one is about VOLUME and the
+other about IDENTITY: a test may store more or less without changing
+what its records are CALLED, and it may share one nominal across its
+choices without changing how much is kept.
+______________________________________________________________________________
+"""
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from enum        import Enum
+
+
+class E_StderrNote(Enum):
+    """WHAT THE BOOK SAYS about a choice's stderr -- and acceptance is
+    where it is written.
+
+    ===================================================================
+    STDERR IS NEVER SUBJECT TO TESTING.
+    ===================================================================
+    stderr is for ERROR REPORTING. That is its whole job. A test
+    application's stderr is never a nominal, never compared, never
+    pype-d.
+
+    Where error reporting is itself the thing under test, the
+    application FLUSHES IT INTO A FILE and names that file in the
+    'output' parameter. A file is a subject; stderr is not.
+
+    WHY: a stream that both carries diagnostics and is held to a
+    blessed text cannot do either job. Every diagnostic an author adds
+    while debugging would fail the test, so the author learns to stay
+    silent -- and the framework has then taught them to remove the
+    reporting it wanted.
+
+    NOMINAL is RETIRED and REMOVED. It is never written by anything --
+    'orchestrator/services/accept.py' promotes every subject EXCEPT
+    stderr, and refuses by name where stderr spoke and no note
+    tolerates it (E-5, services/RATIONALE.txt).
+
+    IGNORED    whatever happens on stderr, DO NOT WORRY: it is never
+               read, never compared, never reported.
+    FORBIDDEN  a word there is an ERROR ('unexpected-stderr'). An
+               UNNOTED choice reads FORBIDDEN: a test nobody was asked
+               about has never spoken there, and its first word is
+               news.
+    """
+    IGNORED   = "ignored"
+    FORBIDDEN = "forbidden"
+
+    def __str__(self):
+        """RETURN: str, the note's token, as the book holds it."""
+        return self.value
+
+
+@dataclass
+class StoreConfig:
+    """WHERE and HOW MUCH is kept. Held verbatim by the test's
+    configuration (README 2.7); 'None' there means: do not record.
+
+    directory      the test directory whose book and records these are.
+                   Nominals live under 'GOOD/', the store's own records
+                   under '.hwut-store/' -- apart from the test's 'OUT/',
+                   which is the test's product space and whose every
+                   file execution reads as a subject.
+
+    record_raw     keep the PRE-CANONICALISATION stream beside the
+                   candidate. Off by default: it doubles the volume and
+                   is wanted only where a canonicaliser is under
+                   suspicion.
+
+    record_timing  keep per-line delta times beside the candidate. Off
+                   by default. Where a provider cannot measure, the
+                   result is 'None' -- never an empty stand-in, never
+                   a zero.
+    """
+    directory:     str
+    record_raw:    bool = False
+    record_timing: bool = False
+
+
+@dataclass
+class NamingConfig:
+    """THE LAW BY WHICH A RECORD IS NAMED.
+
+    same_nominal_f  ALL CHOICES SHARE ONE NOMINAL: the choice part is
+                    dropped from the nominal's key, so 'demo--a.stdout'
+                    and 'demo--b.stdout' are both held against
+                    'demo.stdout'. Stated by the author as 'same' in
+                    the header (exploration's TestParameters, root
+                    only), it saves one blessed file per choice where
+                    every choice must produce the same behaviour.
+
+                    CANDIDATES ARE UNAFFECTED: each choice's own run is
+                    still recorded under its own name, or the choices
+                    would overwrite one another and no diff could name
+                    which choice diverged.
+    """
+    same_nominal_f: bool = False
diff --git a/orchestrator/bookkeeper/stream_store.py b/orchestrator/bookkeeper/stream_store.py
new file mode 100644
--- /dev/null
+++ b/orchestrator/bookkeeper/stream_store.py
@@ -0,0 +1,231 @@
+"""SPDX-License: MIT; Project VUT; (C) Frank-Rene Schaefer
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+PURPOSE
+       THE STORE -- every artifact of a test, behind one interface.
+
+DESCRIPTION
+       Three worlds are kept apart (README 2.7). This is the second: the
+       ARTIFACTS. Records are keyed by (test, choice, subject); the
+       default backend is the HWUT GOOD filesystem, and another -- a
+       database, an object store -- fits behind the same interface.
+
+       THE NAMING IS THE BOOKKEEPER'S. A Store is constructed OVER a
+       Bookkeeper (vut/engine/orchestrator/bookkeeper/) and asks it for
+       every path it touches; the key scheme has ONE expression, there.
+       The Store triggers the reading and the writing; the Bookkeeper
+       answers where. Results and entries are the Bookkeeper's whole
+       business -- nothing of them lives here.
+
+       TWO KEY SPACES, ONE WRITER EACH. A candidate is written by
+       recording; a nominal is written by acceptance. No key has two
+       writers, so 'which step wrote this' never becomes a question a key
+       cannot answer.
+
+       THE DIRECTORY IS THE LOCK. 'mkdir' of a lock sub directory either
+       creates or fails, so the winner is decided without a second
+       mechanism. The lock names its holder as (PROCESS ID, WHEN IT
+       STARTED) -- the PAIR identifies, a process id alone does not, since
+       the system reuses them. Liveness is ASKED, not inferred from
+       elapsed time: no expiry, no heart beat, no clock skew, and a
+       crashed run leaves no lock behind it. The mechanism is
+       'MkdirMutex' (vut/auxiliary/directory_mutex.py); 'DirectoryLock'
+       is its face here, and non-recursive: the live holder locking
+       again is refused by name ('DirectoryDeadlock').
+______________________________________________________________________________
+"""
+import json
+import os
+from   dataclasses import dataclass
+from   pathlib     import Path
+from .configuration import StoreConfig   # noqa: F401
+
+#  The lock mechanism lives in the auxiliary; these names are part of
+#  THIS component's face and are re-exported here.
+from   ....auxiliary.directory_mutex import (MkdirMutex,        # noqa: F401
+                                            DirectoryBusy,
+                                            DirectoryDeadlock,
+                                            liveness_can_be_asked,
+                                            LOCK_DIRECTORY_NAME)
+
+
+LOCK_DIRECTORY_NAME = ".hwut-lock"
+
+
+def _utc_now():
+    """RETURN: str, the current UTC instant, seconds resolution,
+    ISO-8601."""
+    from datetime import datetime, timezone
+    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
+
+
+def source_digest_of(path):
+    """
+    RETURN: str, the sha256 hex digest of the file's CONTENT -- the
+            source identity a freshness sidecar records. Content, not
+            path or mtime: the identity survives a checkout and a
+            touch alike.
+    """
+    import hashlib
+    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
+        return hashlib.sha256(fh.read()).hexdigest()
+_HOLDER_FILE_NAME   = "holder.json"
+
+
+class DirectoryLock(MkdirMutex):
+    """The access check of ONE test directory: it guards the directory's
+    book and its tests' output alike.
+
+    Use as a context manager. Where liveness cannot be asked, the lock is
+    a no-op and '.taken' says so. The face of 'MkdirMutex'
+    (vut/auxiliary/directory_mutex.py) in this component; mechanism,
+    holder record and policies live there.
+    """
+    pass
+
+
+class Store:
+    """The artifacts of the tests under ONE directory.
+
+    Default backend: the HWUT GOOD filesystem. A record is a file under
+    'GOOD/' named for its key; candidates live beside it under 'OUT/'.
+    Constructed OVER a Bookkeeper: the directory is the Bookkeeper's,
+    and every path is asked of it.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, bookkeeper, config=None):
+        self.bookkeeper = bookkeeper
+        self.config     = config
+
+    @property
+    def directory(self):
+        """RETURN: Path, the ONE directory -- the Bookkeeper's."""
+        return Path(self.bookkeeper.directory)
+
+    @property
+    def records(self):
+        """
+        RETURN: True,  a run's subjects are to be kept as candidates.
+                False, nothing is recorded.
+
+        Answered by the presence of a StoreConfig: a configuration that
+        names no store asks for no recording.
+        """
+        return self.config is not None
+
+    # -- keys: THE NAMING IS THE BOOKKEEPER'S -------------------------
+    def stderr_note(self, test, choice):
+        """RETURN: E_StderrNote, what the book says about that choice's
+        stderr; FORBIDDEN where nothing is noted."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.stderr_note(test, choice)
+
+    def note_stderr(self, test, choice, note):
+        """RETURN: E_StderrNote, what now stands in the book."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.note_stderr(test, choice, note)
+
+    def nominal_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the ACCEPTED record of that key lives."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.nominal_path(test, choice, subject)
+
+    def candidate_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the CANDIDATE record of that key lives."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.candidate_path(test, choice, subject)
+
+
+    def write_candidate(self, test, choice, subject, text,
+                        source_digest=None, instant=None):
+        """
+        RETURN: Path, where the candidate was written.
+
+        The candidate key space has ONE writer: recording. Beside every
+        candidate a FRESHNESS sidecar '<candidate>.when' records the
+        instant and the SOURCE IDENTITY -- the digest of the test
+        source's content -- so that a later staleness law can be ruled
+        without re-recording history. A candidate must be COMPLETE: a
+        partial subject of an aborted run is reported, never stored as
+        if whole -- refused at the door by the CALLER, which is the only
+        place completeness is known.
+
+        'instant' is a parameter so that a test may state the clock.
+        """
+        path = self.candidate_path(test, choice, subject)
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+            fh.write(text)
+        freshness = {"instant": instant or _utc_now()}
+        if source_digest is not None:
+            freshness["source_digest"] = source_digest
+        with open(str(path) + ".when", "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+            json.dump(freshness, fh, sort_keys=True)
+        return path
+
+    def accept(self, test, choice, subject, text):
+        """
+        RETURN: Path, where the nominal was written.
+
+        PROMOTION: this is the only way a nominal comes to exist. The
+        nominal key space has ONE writer: acceptance.
+        """
+        path = self.nominal_path(test, choice, subject)
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+            fh.write(text)
+        return path
+
+    def raw_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the PRE-canonicalisation stream lives."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.raw_path(test, choice, subject)
+
+    def timing_path(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """RETURN: Path, where the cadence sidecar of that key lives."""
+        return self.bookkeeper.timing_path(test, choice, subject)
+
+    def write_raw(self, test, choice, subject, text):
+        """
+        RETURN: Path, where the raw stream was written.
+
+        Kept BESIDE the canonicalised record, never instead of it: what
+        is compared is the canonicalised one, and the raw is evidence.
+        """
+        path = self.raw_path(test, choice, subject)
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+            fh.write(text)
+        return path
+
+    def write_timing(self, test, choice, subject, delta_list):
+        """
+        RETURN: Path, where the cadence was written -- one delta per RAW
+                line, in raw order.
+
+        SEPARATE FROM THE RECORD by construction, so the pipe into
+        compare is untouched. Separateness is a technicality of storage:
+        'has this subject a cadence' is answered by 'timing()', and
+        nothing above the Store sees a file.
+        """
+        path = self.timing_path(test, choice, subject)
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+            json.dump({"unit": "second", "delta_list": list(delta_list)}, fh)
+            fh.write("\n")
+        return path
+
+    def timing(self, test, choice, subject):
+        """
+        RETURN: tuple, the recorded per-line deltas of that key.
+                None,  none was recorded.
+
+        NOTE the order: the cadence belongs to the RAW stream while the
+        record is the CANONICALISED one. Where a canonicaliser reorders,
+        the deltas describe the RUN, not the record's lines.
+        """
+        try:
+            with open(self.timing_path(test, choice, subject), "r",
+                      encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+                return tuple(json.load(fh)["delta_list"])
+        except Exception:
+            return None
+
+    def lock(self):
+        """RETURN: DirectoryLock, the access check of this directory."""
+        return DirectoryLock(self.directory)

+disc-1  A FORMAL FORM FOR THE TEST SEQUENCE                     ** OPEN **
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+NOTED, NOT RULED. Written down so it is not lost; nothing here is built and
+nothing here is decided.
+
+THE THOUGHT
+
+The test sequence is a RESULT, not a thing an author writes:
+
+    test case specification  +  test configuration  ->  test sequence
+
+and it may have a small grammar of its own -- a sequence of STEPS, each step
+saying how its members stand to one another:
+
+    parallel [ test0, test1 ]
+    select   [ test1 -> test2 ]
+
+Sketched, not settled. 'parallel' says these may run at once; 'select' names
+an ordering between two. What the full set of step kinds is, and what an
+arrow means exactly, is open.
+
+WHY IT IS WORTH HAVING
+
+A formal test sequence is something 'hwut.parse --test-sequence' can PRINT.
+An author would then see not only what he configured (todo-7) but what the
+framework intends to DO with it -- the schedule itself, before anything
+runs.
+
+WHERE IT MAY LEAD
+
+The note is kept because it looks like it may help considerably later. A
+sequence with a form of its own is a thing that can be printed, diffed,
+recorded, replayed, and reasoned about -- and a scheduler that emits one is
+answerable for its decisions in a way that a scheduler emitting only
+behaviour is not.
+
+WHAT IT STANDS ON
+
+Exploration already settles the inputs: 'collision' says what may not run at
+the same time, 'dependency' says what must have run first, and [MISDEP] says
+what cannot be reached at all (R-32, R-33, R-35, R-36). The sequence is what
+a scheduler makes of those three, and the scheduler is the orchestrator's.
+
+OPEN, all of it:
+  -  the step kinds and their grammar
+  -  whether the form is written down anywhere or exists only in flight
+  -  who owns it: exploration produces the relations, the orchestrator
+     produces the sequence -- so the form is probably the orchestrator's
+     and this note belongs there once that component is opened
+  -  what '--test-sequence' prints when the graph carries a cycle

+disc-2  TREE WALK -- PROVISIONAL LAWS                        ** OPEN **
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+Taken to build R-69; reported, not settled; each one line to change.
+
+ 1  Names beginning '.' are skipped; the walk descends sorted and does
+    NOT descend into a test directory (what stands below one is its
+    own).
+ 2  The ROOT itself is never a test directory unless its own name
+    equals the marker -- 'hwut.plan --directory=X' remains the face
+    for one directory taken as a test directory regardless of name.
+ 3  'language_setup' inherits CHILD-WINS WHOLE, not per language.
+ 4  'ignore' inherits child-wins whole (a child stating 'ignore'
+    replaces the parent's, it does not add to it).

+todo-1  THE 'output' PARAMETER -- SUBJECTS ARE THE TEST'S DESIGN
+        decided, unbuilt
+
+DECIDED
+    A test states what it produces. New header parameter 'output':
+
+        absent          the subject is '<stdout>', and only that
+        a list          the subjects, in order. '<stdout>' is a
+                        SPECIAL name marking the standard output
+                        channel; every other entry is a FILE NAME,
+                        read AFTER the run has ended.
+
+    Example:
+
+        hwut {
+            output = ["<stdout>", "result.csv", "diagnostics.log"]
+        }
+
+    '<stderr>' IS NOT A VALID ENTRY and is refused by name, with the
+    reason (see THE THREE LAWS below).
+
+    WHY CONFIGURATION AND NOT A COMMAND-LINE OPTION: what a test
+    produces is its DESIGN, authored once, not something restated at
+    every acceptance. The three worlds hold -- configuration says what
+    the subjects ARE; the store holds what they WERE; acceptance
+    decides which recording is the pole.
+
+    ===================================================================
+    THE THREE LAWS -- state these in every manual that touches subjects
+    ===================================================================
+
+    (1) STDERR IS NEVER SUBJECT TO TESTING.
+        stderr is for ERROR REPORTING, and that is its whole job. It
+        is never a nominal, never compared. Where error reporting is
+        itself under test, the application FLUSHES IT TO A FILE and
+        names that file in 'output'.
+
+        A stream that both carries diagnostics and is held to a
+        blessed text can do neither job: every diagnostic an author
+        adds while debugging would fail the test, so the author learns
+        to stay silent, and the framework has taught them to remove
+        the reporting it wanted.
+
+    (2) A FILE IS READ ONLY AFTER THE TEST HAS TERMINATED.
+        Therefore file content is NOT subject to race conditions --
+        there is no interleaving to observe, no flush to time, no
+        FIFO-sink question. This is why a file, and not a stream, is
+        the answer to (1).
+
+    (3) PYPE-ING IS ONLY EVER APPLIED TO STDOUT.
+        Not to stderr (never read), not to files (not a stream; read
+        whole, after the end). 'canonicalisers' is a dict keyed by
+        subject and the bookkeeper iterates it generically, but
+        'stdout' is the ONLY key that will ever stand in it. The
+        generality is harmless; the law is what governs.
+
+    WHAT ACCEPT STILL DECIDES -- one thing, and only for stderr:
+
+        (B) stderr silent          -> FORBIDDEN, silently, the default
+        (A) stderr speaks          -> REFUSE. No default: a default
+                                      here leaves a user clueless in
+                                      front of output that deviates.
+                                      Request '--stderr-tol[erated]',
+                                      which notes IGNORED.
+
+        There is no second remedy. 'stderr becomes nominal' is dropped.
+
+BUILT (this session) -- the parameter is LIVE end to end
+    exploration     'output' in KEY_TO_FIELD, TestParameters,
+                    validator ('<stderr>' refused by name with the
+                    E-5 reason; '<stdin>' and every other unknown
+                    channel refused; duplicates refused; faults
+                    position-accurate). ConfigRunner OWES it, default
+                    ('<stdout>',); RELATION carries the row -- the
+                    orphan contract (test-defaults) holds.
+    adapter         '<stdout>' -> 'stdout'; file names verbatim; onto
+                    'TestChoiceConfiguration.output'.
+    session         the declaration GOVERNS the judged subjects; an
+                    explicit request still speaks.
+    stage_execute   declared files read AFTER termination -- and then
+                    REMOVED. READ-AND-REMOVE was decided DURING the
+                    build, forced by two live findings: a lingering
+                    file is explored as a candidate test application
+                    by the next walk (seen: the explorer interviewing
+                    a CSV), and a STALE file from run N greens run
+                    N+1 even where the application stopped producing
+                    it -- a false green. The store's record is the
+                    product; the file was only the carrier (the sink
+                    law of multi_execute._read_sinks). The former
+                    'OUT/' DISCOVERY sweep is RETIRED: discovery can
+                    never tell 'produced' from 'forgot', so the
+                    verdict 'output-file-not-found' could never fire.
+    accept          a file subject is promoted like stdout; proven:
+                    declare -> run -> bless -> green -> forget the
+                    file -> 'output file missing', exit 1.
+    suite           exploration/TEST/test-output.sh: declare, refuse,
+                    cycle, forgotten -- blessed.
+
+STILL OPEN of the original list
+    (4) accept/merge  '--stderr-tol' is BUILT (accept); merge's
+                      stderr handling follows when merge is built
+    (5) the manual    the three laws into every manual that touches
+                      subjects -- exploration README pending
+
+RETIRED BY THIS RULING -- its own piece of work
+    'E_StderrNote.NOMINAL' is dead. It still stands in the enum
+    because these read or write it:
+
+        orchestrator/bookkeeper/bookkeeper.py:393   the removal branch
+        operations/consume/accept.py:152,156        a match arm, and a
+                                                    note_stderr write
+        operations/session.py:279                   judged_list gains
+                                                    'stderr'
+        operations/consume/TEST/test-accept.py      a BLESSED suite
+                                                    that iterates all
+                                                    four note states
+
+    Removing the member moves that GOOD, so it is a step of its own
+    and not a side effect of building 'output'.

+todo-1  OWNER FACES: WHAT EACH COMPONENT OWES
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-37..R-43). The mechanism is BUILT: 'relation.py' relates our word
+to '(class, member)', writes values with setattr, reads defaults out of the
+frozen dataclass declaration, and checks the whole table at IMPORT.
+
+UNBUILT: the owners have not declared their faces.
+'owner_faces_provisional.py' stands in for all six.
+
+WHAT AN OWNER OWES (R-41, R-57): its configuration declared as a FROZEN
+DATACLASS, with the default it judges least astonishing on each member --
+its own members, in its own words. No face, no second declaration: where a
+word of ours is several members of its, an ADAPTER in 'relation.py' bridges
+the two, and the default is derived backwards through it.
+
+    ConfigBuild          -> the build component
+    ConfigCaps           -> procsitter          (todo-4 names the caps)
+    ConfigCanonicalise   -> the pype component
+    ConfigCompare        -> vut.engine.compare  (below)
+    ConfigStore          -> the result store    ('same', R-45)
+    ConfigRunner         -> the runner          ('interactive', R-46)
+
+
+COMPARE: WHAT IS THERE TODAY
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+Compare's configuration is not flat and not frozen:
+
+    Configuration                       slotted, mutable
+        constraint_db                   dict, top level
+        pattern_finder                  ConfigurationPatternFinder,
+                                        NOT slotted
+            numeric_tolerance_ratio
+            equivalent_pattern_list
+            visible_nothing_pattern_list
+            analogy_f
+            analogy_begin_marker
+            analogy_end_marker
+            constraint_f
+            whitespace_f
+            backslash_f
+
+THE FACE COMPARE OWES, member by member, against our vocabulary:
+
+    numeric        -> numeric_tolerance_ratio        (0.0)
+    eq-pattern     -> equivalent_pattern_list        (())
+    nothing        -> visible_nothing_pattern_list   (())
+    analogy        -> the marker PAIR (R-24), which compare holds as
+                      three fields: 'analogy_f' plus two markers. The
+                      face carries ONE member -- a pair, empty for off.
+    constraints    -> the expression LIST (R-25), which compare holds as
+                      'constraint_f' plus 'constraint_db', a NAMED map.
+                      The face carries one member: a tuple.
+    slash          -> backslash_f
+    comment        -> compare's comment marker, wherever it lives today
+
+SETTLED (R-54, R-55, R-56):
+
+  -  'whitespace' entered the vocabulary; it relates to compare's
+     'whitespace_f'. 'slash' relates to 'backslash_f'.
+  -  'constraints' stays a LIST; compare relates each expression to its
+     variables itself.
+  -  Compare's configuration becomes frozen dataclasses. The FACE is
+     no longer asked for (R-57 supersedes R-40's face): three adapters
+     -- ANALOGY, CONSTRAINTS, COMMENT -- bridge our words to compare's
+     own members, and 'ConfigCompare' in
+     'owner_faces_provisional.py' now MIRRORS compare's real
+     'ConfigurationPatternFinder' member for member.
+
+STILL OPEN, and only compare can answer:
+
+  SETTLED SINCE (R-64, R-65, R-66):
+
+  a  'comment' is a marker PAIR, read like 'analogy'. Compare therefore
+     needs an 'ignored_line_f' beside its two markers, as it has
+     'analogy_f' beside those two -- otherwise OFF cannot be said. It
+     is in the delivered 'configuration.py'.
+  b  'strip_whitespace_f' stays compare's own; no specification reaches
+     it. 'whitespace_eqv' is the whitespace parameter and the only one.
+  c  The constraint namespace carries the maths and 'glob', and a test
+     run has ONE for all its constraints:
+     'engine/constraint_namespace.py' in the delivered bundle, with
+     'engine/README-constraint_namespace.txt' beside it.
+
+  STILL OPEN, in compare: 'engine/constraints.py' already declares
+  'SAFE_FUNCTION_DB', 'SAFE_CONSTANT_DB' and 'SAFE_STR_METHOD_SET' of its
+  own, and its 'ConstraintSpace' is the run-scoped half of the namespace.
+  Those three names become imports from 'constraint_namespace':
+
+      SAFE_FUNCTION_DB    -> FUNCTION_DB
+      SAFE_CONSTANT_DB    -> CONSTANT_DB
+      SAFE_STR_METHOD_SET -> STR_METHOD_SET
+
+  and the scope built in 'ConstraintExpression' becomes
+  'namespace_of(bindings)'. The AST allow-list stays exactly as it is.
+
+
+THE MOVE, per owner:
+  1  The owner declares its face.
+  2  'RELATION' names the owner's class; member names change with it.
+  3  The stand-in class is deleted; when the file is empty, it goes.
+
+Nothing else changes, and '_assert_relation()' fails loudly at import if a
+name does not match. 'test-defaults.py' prints every declared default, so
+each step's GOOD file change IS the contract change.
+
+THE PROVISIONAL VALUES were not ruled one by one. One is worth the owner's
+eye: 'caps.network = True' -- the stand-in permits network access, and the
+opposite is defensible. It is procsitter's call.

+todo-2  INSTANTIATION: THE FACES BLESSED ON REAL COMPONENTS
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-18, R-37..R-41). BUILT: 'instantiate.configurations_of' yields one
+COMPLETE configuration per owning class, with no per-component code -- the
+relation table is the whole mechanism.
+
+UNBUILT: the blessing against the REAL components. 'test-defaults.py
+instantiate' records what the STAND-IN faces yield
+(owner_faces_provisional.py), and its GOOD file is therefore a description of
+the stand-in, not of compare.
+
+That is deliberate and it is safe here, because the stand-in's values are
+never the oracle of a comparison: the choice prints what the mechanism
+produced, and the mechanism is what is under test.
+
+COMPARE IN PARTICULAR: 'instantiate.configurations_of' hands compare an
+instance of its FACE, not of its live 'Configuration'. Building the live one
+from the face is compare's step, and the place where 'analogy' becomes
+'analogy_f' plus two markers, and 'constraints' becomes 'constraint_f' plus
+a map. That translation is compare's to write and compare's to test; this
+component states the author's words and nothing more.
+
+AT INTEGRATION, per owner (see todo-1):
+  1  'RELATION' names the owner's class.
+  2  Run 'test-defaults.py declared' and 'instantiate'; INSPECT the first
+     output before blessing it. A member the owner renamed shows in
+     'declared' as "DECLARES NOTHING" or as a missing member, by name.
+  3  Record the GOOD files against the real components, in one batch, once
+     all four faces exist -- not one at a time, which would bless three
+     stand-ins alongside one real face and hide which is which.

+todo-3  '--hwut-info' IS THE THIRD CARRIER (MIGRATION)
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-44). BUILT, except the procsitter call.
+
+THE ORDER, per candidate file. Nothing changes for a file that carries a
+header or stands under 'apps':
+
+    file not ignored
+        |
+        +-- named under 'apps' in hwut.conf?      -> CONF carrier
+        |
+        +-- 'hwut {' trigger in the file?         -> HEADER carrier
+        |
+        +-- neither: it may be an hwut 1.0 test application.
+            INTERVIEW it:  'app --hwut-info'      -> INTERVIEW carrier
+
+'otto-von-bismarck.bas' has no trigger because it predates the trigger. The
+interview is what an hwut 1.0 application has always answered, and it is
+read as a specification.
+
+WHAT IS READ. Only what hwut 1.0 defines, as 'HwutRunner' emits it:
+
+    <title>;                    -> title
+    CHOICES: <name>, ...;       -> choices, empty bodies
+    HAPPY: <pattern>;           -> eq-pattern (R-12: 'happy' was renamed)
+    SAME;                       -> same        (R-45)
+    INTERACTIVE;                -> interactive  (R-46)
+
+Nothing else is looked for. A line the block carries that hwut 1.0 does not
+define is passed over in silence, not faulted: this is a migration path and
+an old application owes us nothing.
+
+CONSEQUENCE, worth stating plainly: exploration EXECUTES. It was a function
+of the directory's text alone (R-1, R-19); with the interview it runs a
+program -- once per header-less, unnamed candidate, and only for those. The
+cost of the static specification was computed choices; the interview buys
+them back for legacy files without buying them back for new ones.
+
+  -  the interview runs under procsitter, with caps: an old application
+     that hangs must not hang exploration.
+  -  a non-zero exit, a timeout, or an unreadable answer means: this file
+     is NOT a test application. Silence is not a fault (R-2).
+  -  what comes back is a specification like any other, marked
+     'E_Origin.INTERVIEW', and it resolves and runs like any other.
+
+DONE:
+  1  'hwut_info_interview.py'. 'specification_of(text, name)' reads the
+     block; 'interview(directory, name, runner)' asks and reads. The
+     answer becomes a TestAppSpec with 'E_Origin.INTERVIEW'; every way
+     of failing to be a test application yields None, which is no fault.
+  2  'explorer.explore' takes the third branch where both carriers are
+     silent, and 'interview_runner' is a parameter so a test may drive
+     it without a process.
+  3  'INTERVIEW_CAPS' -- timeout_sec 10, network false,
+     child_process_max_n 1. An interview is a question, not a test: it
+     may not spend and it may not reach.
+  4  TEST/test-interview.py: block, silence, directory, precedence.
+
+OWED: '_procsitter_runner' raises NotImplementedError. The call itself is
+written at integration, when procsitter is in the tree, and the GOOD file of
+a choice that exercises it is recorded THEN -- against the real procsitter,
+never against a stand-in.
+  2  'explorer': the third branch, reached only where both carriers are
+     silent.
+  3  The block reader: loose, tolerant, and looking only for the lines
+     hwut 1.0 defines.
+  4  TEST: a directory holding a 1.0-style application; the interview
+     read; a file that answers nothing; a file that exits non-zero; a
+     file that hangs and is capped.
+
+OPEN: nothing blocking. 'hwut.parse' shows the ORIGIN of each value --
+"default", "--hwut-info", and the rest -- which is recorded in todo-7.

+todo-4  THE VOCABULARY OF 'caps'
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-22): 'caps' is procsitter's scope, and procsitter owns every
+default in it. DECIDED (R-47): the vocabulary is FIXED -- an unknown cap is
+a fault at the door. DECIDED (R-48): a cap procsitter cannot enforce refuses
+the test.
+
+BUILT:  timeout_sec, network.
+
+NAMED, UNBUILT -- each is one field in 'Caps', one branch in
+'validator._caps', and one entry in 'RELATION':
+
+    cpu_sec               CPU time, as distinct from wall clock
+    memory_mb             address space
+    file_size_mb          the largest file the process may write
+    child_process_max_n   how many children it may spawn
+    file_handle_max_n     how many handles it may hold open
+    write_directory_list  where it may write. DEFAULT: the directory of
+                          the test source and all of its
+                          sub-directories.
+
+Most map onto 'setrlimit' and are cheap. 'network' and
+'write_directory_list' do not -- they need namespaces or a sandbox, and
+procsitter may be unable to enforce them on a given platform.
+
+WHERE A CAP CANNOT BE ENFORCED (R-48): the test does not run. The
+documentation must state the way out:
+
+    --ignore=<cap>    run the test with that cap unenforced, knowingly
+
+The switch belongs to the RUNNER, not to this component. It is written down
+here so that it is not lost: a refusal with no stated remedy is a wall.
+
+OWED TO PROCSITTER (todo-1): the defaults of every cap above, declared on
+its own face. The values in 'owner_faces_provisional.ConfigCaps' are
+stand-ins and were not ruled one by one.
diff --git a/orchestrator/exploration/DISCUSSIONS/todo-5-directory-keys-rebuilt.txt b/orchestrator/exploration/DISCUSSIONS/todo-5-directory-keys-rebuilt.txt

@@ -0,0 +1,53 @@
+todo-5  THE DIRECTORY KEYS, REBUILT
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-31..R-36). BUILT. This file records what was done and what is
+left open for the orchestrator.
+
+    old                     new
+    ---                     ---
+    enter                   on_entry
+    leave                   on_exit
+    parallel, isolated      collision   = [ <target>, ... ]
+    sequence                dependency  { <target> = [ <target>, ... ] }
+
+A TARGET is a file name, or a file name and a choice name with one blank
+between; a bare file name means every choice of that file.
+
+DONE:
+  1  'DirectorySpec': rename two fields, strike three, add two.
+  2  'validator._directory_key': the two new readers, and the target
+     reader they share. A target naming a file or choice that neither
+     carrier offers is refused AT EXPLORATION -- both carriers are read
+     there, so it is knowable, and it is the same class of fault as the
+     ghost 'apps' entry (E_FaultKind.DIRECTORY).
+  3  Satisfiability over the graph, computed at exploration: a case in a
+     cycle, depending on a cycle member, depending on a [MISDEP] case, or
+     naming a target that does not exist, carries [MISDEP]. The token is
+     a property of the CASE and belongs on 'CTestCase'.
+  4  The cycle itself, named in the directory failure's message.
+  5  TEST: 'test-explorer.py graph' -- targets that exist, a target the
+     directory does not offer, and a cycle with two cases hanging off it,
+     all five carrying [MISDEP] while the fault names the three that
+     form the cycle. 'test-validator.py conf' carries the keys;
+     'test-task_list.py all' shows the token on a selected case.
+
+FOR THE ORCHESTRATOR -- what the two relations MEAN, so that nothing has to
+be inferred from the keys:
+
+    collision    a group of test runs -- a test application together with
+                 its choices -- MAY NOT RUN IN PARALLEL. Every member of
+                 a collision group runs alone with respect to the others.
+                 What is named in no group may run in parallel with
+                 anything.
+
+    dependency   what it says: the named targets must have RUN before
+                 this one starts. An ordering relation and not a success
+                 relation (R-36) -- the verdict of a dependency does not
+                 enter, and a dependant runs once its dependencies have
+                 run to completion.
+
+Exploration states the relations and settles satisfiability ([MISDEP],
+R-35). HOW the orchestrator schedules what remains -- the order it picks
+among the free ones, how many run at once -- is its own ruling, and
+disc-1-test-sequence-form.txt is where that begins.

+todo-6  THE PARSER MOVES TO 'language_support/python'
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-27, R-34). BUILT. This file records the move and what remains
+open after it.
+
+    vut/language_support/python/
+        hwut_runner.py
+        hocon_parser.py        <-- one copy, shipped to test authors
+
+    vut/engine/orchestrator/exploration/reader.py
+        imports it from there
+
+DONE:
+  1  The file moved; the module kept its name.
+  2  The parser states its OWN fault record ('ParseFault', 'E_ParseFault')
+     and imports nothing from the engine; 'reader._converted' says it in
+     the engine's vocabulary. 'SourceLine' is the parser's and the
+     unwrapper imports it.
+  3  The parser's TEST choices moved with it, GOOD files and all.
+  4  What stayed: the detector, the unwrapper, the validator -- the
+     unwrapper knows about comment leaders, which is HWUT's business and
+     not HOCON's.
+
+NOT NECESSARY FOR NOW: the other languages. The Python parser is what HWUT
+itself uses, and a port is written when a language actually needs one --
+'language_support/<lang>/' as the heart desires. The GOOD files of the
+Python one are the description any port is measured against, and they exist
+already. Nothing waits on this.

+todo-7  THE 'hwut.parse' SERVICE
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-9, R-29). BUILT, except the command line itself.
+
+    hwut.parse <source file>    scan for the trigger token, extract the
+                                specification, check, resolve, print
+    hwut.parse                  act as the explorer: 'hwut.conf' first,
+                                then the files
+
+It checks the syntax, RESOLVES the defaults, and prints the complete
+configuration, prettily -- what the framework READ, not what was written.
+
+DONE:
+  1  'printer.py'. It walks 'KEY_TO_FIELD' and 'RELATION', so a
+     parameter added to the vocabulary appears here without a line
+     being written -- the second rendering follows the first by
+     construction.
+  2  PROVENANCE per value ('provenance.py'), settled at resolution
+     while the three sources still stand apart, and annotated in a
+     HOCON comment only where the value is not this choice's own word:
+     'app', 'hwut.conf:<line>', '--hwut-info', 'default' (R-51, R-53).
+  2b '--no-default' drops every line that reads 'default'; a scope
+     whose every leaf would go is dropped whole.
+  2d '--provenance' names the PLACE of every stated value; '--gnu'
+     names it in the GNU error format, 'file:line:column' (R-67).
+  2c The printed form IS the specification language (R-50): what is
+     printed can be read back.
+  3  'hwut_parse.text_of_file' and 'text_of_directory'. A file whose
+     specification carries a fault yields NO tree and its faults
+     instead: the service shows what exploration will do, and
+     exploration refuses rather than guesses.
+  4  TEST/test-printer.py: file, origins, directory, faults.
+
+OWED:
+  a  The command line itself -- 'hwut.parse [<file>]' -- which belongs
+     with the other services, not inside this component.
+  b  The extraction shown: which region was found, at which line, what
+     leader was stripped. An author whose header does not parse needs
+     that before he needs the tree.
+  c  '--test-sequence' (disc-1): the schedule the framework intends.
+
+  5  '--test-sequence': print the schedule the framework intends, not
+     only the configuration it read. The form of that sequence is open
+     and noted in disc-1-test-sequence-form.txt; the switch is named
+     here so the service is not built in a shape that cannot carry it.
+
+OPEN: the printed form itself. It is a face an author reads daily and is
+worth drawing before it is built.

+todo-8  'same' AND 'interactive' ENTER THE VOCABULARY
+______________________________________________________________________________
+
+DECIDED (R-45, R-46). BUILT. This file records what was done and what
+remains owed to the owners.
+
+    same         all choices of this file share ONE nominal file --
+                 a storage economy.
+    interactive  the application serves a session: choices driven over
+                 stdin, several per call, no restart per choice.
+
+Both are booleans and both stand AT THE ROOT ONLY. Neither is a statement a
+single choice can make: 'same' is about the set of choices, 'interactive' is
+about the file's front end.
+
+DONE:
+  1  'KEY_TO_FIELD' carries both; 'TestParameters' has the two boolean
+     fields; 'ROOT_ONLY_KEY_SET' names them in one place.
+  2  The validator refuses either inside a choice, by name, saying why:
+     "'same' stands at the root; it is a statement about every choice of
+     the file, and choice 'one' cannot make it". They are the FIRST
+     exception to one-grammar-unit (README 2).
+  3  'RELATION' carries two entries against two new provisional faces:
+         same         -> ConfigStore.same_nominal_f
+         interactive  -> ConfigRunner.interactive_f
+  4  TEST: 'test-validator.py root_only' -- both at the root reaching
+     every choice, both refused inside one.
+
+OWED (todo-1): the two new faces are stand-ins like the others. The store
+declares whether one nominal per file is its default; the runner already
+carries an 'interactive' flag on 'TestConfiguration' and its member name
+may differ from 'interactive_f'.
+
+NOTE: with these two, the interview's vocabulary (todo-3) is complete --
+every line hwut 1.0's info block carries has a counterpart.
diff --git a/orchestrator/exploration/RATIONALE.txt b/orchestrator/exploration/RATIONALE.txt
new file mode 100644
--- /dev/null
+++ b/orchestrator/exploration/RATIONALE.txt
@@ -0,0 +1,1273 @@
+==============================================================================
+exploration -- RATIONALE
+==============================================================================
+Settled decisions. Read-only.
+
+
+R-1  SPECIFICATION IN THE FILE, NOT IN THE PROCESS
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   HWUT 1.0 discovered a test by executing it and reading
+          '--hwut-info'. Discovery required running every candidate,
+          including malformed ones; the failure mode of a broken
+          candidate at discovery time was murky.
+DECISION  The specification is a static declaration carried by the file.
+          Discovery is a text scan; no execution.
+COST      Computed choices are gone. An application can no longer
+          generate its choice list at run time.
+NOTE      A migration service may translate an HWUT 1.0 application into
+          one carrying a header.
+GRAVEYARD Keeping '--hwut-info' as the oracle: retains the drift-free
+          property, keeps execution-at-discovery and its murky failures.
+
+
+R-2  '--hwut-info' SURVIVES AS A HINT SOURCE
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Header and implementation can drift: the header declares a
+          choice the code does not implement.
+DECISION  Where an application answers, its choices are compared against
+          the specification and the difference feeds error message
+          hints. The protocol is not imposed; absence of an answer is
+          not a fault.
+COST      Drift in an application that does not answer surfaces only at
+          run time.
+GRAVEYARD Imposing the protocol: reintroduces execution as a
+          precondition of exploration.
+
+
+R-3  HOCON
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   The specification is a tree. One language must serve both
+          carriers.
+DECISION  HOCON.
+COST      No HOCON parser in the Python standard library.
+GRAVEYARD INI / getpot style: flat; nesting faked by dotted section
+          names; lists are comma strings re-parsed by hand; every value
+          a string.
+
+
+R-4  ALL LOWERCASE; A STRUCTURAL FENCE, NOT A CASE FENCE
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Framework keys and user-chosen names share one namespace. An
+          earlier ruling separated them by case -- UPPERCASE for the
+          framework -- and 'HWUT-TITLE' was the result.
+DECISION  All lowercase. 'hwut' is the marker, 'title' the required key.
+          The namespace is kept clear structurally: a user-chosen name
+          never stands beside a framework key; it stands inside one that
+          holds only such names ('choices', 'language-setup', 'apps').
+COST      A framework key that admitted free-form names at its own level
+          would break the scheme. None does; none may.
+NOTE      Case distinction is not a property of every writing system, so
+          it cannot carry meaning in a language meant to be written
+          everywhere.
+GRAVEYARD Case as the fence: reads as shouting, and rests on an
+          ethnocentric premise about alphabets.
+
+
+R-5  THE ROOT IS THE DEFAULT; A CHOICE OVERWRITES
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Where does a parameter stand that holds for every choice?
+DECISION  Every test parameter may stand at the root as the default; a
+          choice's own value overwrites it. 'title', 'language' and
+          'choices' are structural and do not participate.
+HISTORY   Ruled the other way first: with 'choices' present, a
+          choice-related key at the root was refused, on the ground that
+          a reader of an empty choice body would have to look upward to
+          learn what it runs. R-6 dissolved that ground -- the choice
+          name IS the call, so nothing at the root can change what a
+          choice runs, only how it is built and how its output is judged.
+COST      A reader of a choice body must look up to the root to know the
+          effective parameters. Resolution therefore happens once, at
+          exploration, so that no consumer ever repeats it.
+
+
+R-6  THE CHOICE NAME IS THE CALL
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   How does a test application learn what to do?
+DECISION  From its first argument, which is the choice name. No 'args'
+          key stands beside a choice name.
+COST      A test instruction weaving extra arguments into a call is not
+          expressible. Parked.
+NOTE      This is what admits the list form 'choices = [one, two]': a
+          choice needing no parameters is a name and nothing else.
+
+
+R-7  ONE FILE, ONE CARRIER
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Header and 'hwut.conf' can both describe the same file.
+DECISION  Disjoint. A file with a header is specified by its header;
+          naming it under 'apps' is a TEST DIRECTORY ERROR. 'hwut.conf'
+          describes only header-less files -- languages without
+          comments, generated code, files whose comment convention the
+          framework cannot know.
+COST      A new error category, TEST DIRECTORY ERROR, distinct from a
+          test's verdict. Separate reporting: parked.
+DERIVED   The root of 'hwut.conf' carries no test parameters for the
+          directory at large: a directory-wide default would silently
+          reach into files it may not touch.
+GRAVEYARD Key-by-key override: the header no longer shows the whole
+          specification. Whole-file override: 'hwut.conf' entries die
+          silently.
+
+
+R-8  NOTHING ABOVE THE TEST DIRECTORY
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   A tree-wide configuration layer would place a TEST directory's
+          meaning outside itself.
+DECISION  A TEST directory is testable standing alone. Two carriers,
+          both inside it. No upward walk, no invocation layer.
+COST      A setting wanted by forty directories is written forty times.
+MITIGATED 'hwut.propagate global.conf' webs shared sections into each
+          directory's 'hwut.conf' as text, at authoring time. Afterwards
+          every directory still says everything about itself; the global
+          file exists at authoring time, never at run time.
+DERIVED   Propagation touches 'hwut.conf' only. A header is therefore
+          never machine-written, which is why its shape must stay
+          comfortable to type: the list form of 'choices', root
+          defaults, and no required key but 'title'.
+OPEN      Test-task descriptions with a global touch: parked.
+
+
+R-9  THE REGION IS FOUND WITHOUT KNOWING THE LANGUAGE
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   The specification stands in a comment, and comment syntax
+          differs per language.
+DECISION  The region is delimited by 'hwut' and its matching brace,
+          found at the first occurrence anywhere in the file. Its lines'
+          longest common leading prefix is stripped; a trailing closer
+          on the last line is tolerated. No table of comment syntaxes
+          exists.
+COST      A file mentioning 'hwut {' in a string or a comment ABOUT hwut
+          is detected. A region whose lines share no common prefix
+          leaves a stray leader in the HOCON; HOCON's own comment syntax
+          ('#', '//') heals those cases, '*'-led and '--'-led ones it
+          does not.
+MITIGATED 'hwut.parse' shows the author what was extracted and how it
+          parsed.
+GRAVEYARD An extension-to-comment-syntax table: a language HWUT has not
+          heard of would not work, which contradicts language
+          agnosticism. A start/end marker pair: one more line to write,
+          and the content is HOCON, whose braces already delimit.
+          Stopping the scan at the end of a 'header': "header" cannot be
+          defined without the comment syntax this ruling removes.
+
+
+R-10  'language' AND 'language-setup' ARE TWO WORDS
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   One word would have meant both "which language is this file
+          written in" and "how does HWUT deal with that language".
+DECISION  'language' is a file's property and stands in either carrier.
+          'language-setup' is the directory's and stands in 'hwut.conf'
+          alone. A source file may hint its language; it may not
+          configure one.
+COST      Two words to learn where one was tempting.
+NOTE      A language with no 'language-setup' entry is called by its own
+          name.
+
+
+R-11  'build' AND 'app'
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   A build command says how to build; what to run afterwards must
+          also be known, and parsing it out of the command works only
+          while the artefact appears literally in it.
+DECISION  'build' is the command, '%' expanding to the source file's
+          stem. 'app' names the artefact and defaults to that stem.
+COST      Two keys where one usually suffices; 'app' is redundant in the
+          ordinary case.
+GRAVEYARD Deriving the artefact from the command: 'make all',
+          'cmake --build . -t thing' and './configure && make' name
+          nothing HWUT could take.
+
+
+R-12  'happy' IS 'eq-pattern'
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   HWUT 1.0's 'happy' and compare's equivalence pattern are one
+          mechanism under two names.
+DECISION  'eq-pattern'. 'happy' is dropped.
+NOTE      The name states what it does: two elements matching one
+          pattern are equivalent, so 'bonjour|hello' accepts either as
+          the greeting.
+
+
+R-13  POSITIONS ARE FILE-RELATIVE FROM THE START
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   An error must name where it occurred. The unwrapper hands the
+          parser a text that is not the text on disk: the line is
+          shifted by the region's offset, the column by the stripped
+          prefix's width.
+DECISION  The unwrapper carries both offsets forward and the parser
+          attaches file-relative positions. No position anywhere in the
+          system is region-relative.
+COST      The unwrapper's output is a text plus two offset series, not a
+          bare string.
+GRAVEYARD Translating at report time: every error path must remember to
+          do it, and one that forgets reports a position that looks
+          plausible and is wrong.
+
+
+R-14  POSITION PER NODE, NOT PER VALUE
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Where is a position attached?
+DECISION  Every key, every value and every block carries one.
+COST      The parsed tree is not plain data.
+RATIONALE The validator's characteristic errors are ABOUT KEYS: an
+          unknown key, a misspelt key, a key in the wrong carrier, a
+          required key absent. A position per value can report none of
+          them -- a misspelt key's only position is its value's, and an
+          absent required key has no value at all.
+
+
+R-15  TWO TREES; THE VALIDATOR IS THE ONLY CONSUMER OF THE FIRST
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Position-carrying nodes force every reader to unwrap a
+          '.value', all the way downstream.
+DECISION  The annotated tree lives between parser and validator and
+          nowhere else. On acceptance the validator emits the plain
+          tree. Nothing downstream knows the annotated tree existed.
+COST      One recursive conversion walk at the validator's exit.
+NOTE      This also settles resolution: root defaults are written into
+          choices after the wrapper is gone, so no synthesised value has
+          to invent a position or carry a false one.
+
+
+R-16  TYPED, SLOTTED, FROZEN RECORDS
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   A plain dict downstream returns the string key, and a typo
+          there is a run-time KeyError -- the same fault class todo-12
+          records against compare's pattern finder.
+DECISION  The plain tree is built of typed, slotted, frozen dataclasses.
+          The vocabulary is stated once, in code; the validator checks
+          against it.
+COST      Resolution cannot mutate in place; it builds new records.
+NOTE      That cost is a benefit: the unresolved and resolved forms
+          cannot be confused, and a consumer holding a 'CTestCase' knows
+          nothing has edited it since.
+
+
+R-17  UNSTATED IS 'None'; ONE DEFAULT TABLE, POINTING TO OWNERS
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   'numeric = 0' stated and 'numeric' never written are different
+          facts. Both need a value at the point of use.
+DECISION  An unstated field is 'None'. This component owns one table
+          mapping a parameter name to the module that owns it; the
+          module states the default. The table's keys are the parameter
+          vocabulary, so vocabulary and defaults are one list.
+COST      A value now has two possible origins, so "why did this test
+          tolerate 1%?" has two answers. 'hwut.parse' shows the
+          effective value with its provenance.
+RATIONALE A default is the value that causes the least astonishment, and
+          only the component that knows what the parameter means can
+          judge that. This component knows 'comment' is a string; it
+          does not know that '##' is what a reader expects.
+NOTE      Adaptability of the table is deferred, not designed for.
+GRAVEYARD A registry each component writes into at import time: a
+          component not imported has not registered, and the table is
+          silently short an entry. Literals restated here: two copies of
+          one fact, the shape 'compare_setup_delta' exists to avoid.
+
+
+R-18  THIS COMPONENT KNOWS THE TABLE; THE EXPLORER INSTANTIATES
+______________________________________________________________________________
+PROBLEM   Who turns absence into a value?
+DECISION  This component owns the table and only knows it. The explorer
+          builds the consumer configurations from record plus table, and
+          keeps the record beside them.
+COST      Two things exist per choice where one would do.
+RATIONALE The record says what the author CHOSE; the configuration says
+          what will happen. The store records the record, so a default
+          setting adds nothing to an entry and a deliberate one is
+          recorded the day it is used -- which is the existing recording
+          law.
